# People tables

For review (shared tables brief, M2), as corrected after the review (brief §12). Every list below is in `src/data/people.json` and is read by the filler once it is wired in (M5, M6). The second-tier cultures (M3) follow the core ones.

Reading the lists: names run from most to least common; the number in brackets is the weight. `ʳ` marks a name kept mainly for a royal bearer, `ˢ` for a saint's. On `modern` given names and on Cornish `historic` names the era tags follow the weight: `ST` (steam age), `MO` (modern), `ST+MO` (both), `MD` (medieval), `EM` (late medieval and early modern). Spelling is the usual English one, one spelling per name.

## Sizes against the brief (§3.1)

Sizes are minimums (brief §12): a table may be larger when a built-in template is.

# Second-tier cultures (M3)

The same rules as the core (brief §12) apply to every list. Weights follow list order (10, 7, 5, 3, 2). Every `modern` given name carries `ST`, `MO` or both.

## Slot forms (brief §3.4)

`{g}` a given name, `{s}` a surname, `{t}` an official's title, `{r}` a ruler form filled with a regnal name, `{rm}` and `{rf}` a regnal name alone, `{h}` a holy form filled with a holy name. Where a period has no surnames, the second column applies.

| Slot (labels that use it) | With surnames | Without surnames | Sex of the given name |
|---|---|---|---|
| founder (founder, explorer, commander, conqueror) | `{s}` 60, `{g} {s}` 15, `{g}` 25 | `{g}` 100 | male 90, female 10 |
| official (official, patron, sponsor, benefactor) | `{s}` 50, `{t} {s}` 30, `{g} {s}` 20 | `{t} {g}` 50, `{g}` 50 | male 90, female 10 |
| person (person, personal name, hero, owner, leader, captain, admiral, president, boss, author, smith, lord, sire, alpha) | `{g}` 85, `{s}` 15 | `{g}` 100 | male 70, female 30 |
| monarch (monarch, ruler, sultan, royal) | `{r}` 50, `{rm}` 50 | the same | male |
| royalWoman (royal woman) | `{rf}` 70, "Queen {rf}" 30 | the same | female |
| dynasty | `{s}` 100 | `{g}` 100 | – |
| house | `{s}` 70, "House of {s}" 30 | "House of {g}" 100 | – |
| holy (holy person, prophet) | `{h}` 100 | the same | – |
| givenMale (name, father, man's name), givenFemale (woman's name), given (child) | `{gm}`, `{gf}` or `{g}` 100 | the same | given: male 50, female 50 |

The extra labels (benefactor, hero, owner and the rest) are the ones the M1 label sweep found beyond the brief's table; each reads the slot form shown. The sex shares and the "without surnames" forms are my choices; the brief fixes only the "with surnames" column for the first five slots.

Titles for officials (`{t}`) come from the titles module's office kind in the culture's bynames culture: Anglo-Saxon and Norman & British read their own; Welsh, Scottish, Irish and Cornish read Celtic; Scandinavian reads Norse; German reads General (the bynames module has no German list). The filler resolves the title at fill time from `officeWord` and `ranks` in `bynames.json`.

## How a culture, region and era pick a table

- **Period:** AN, MD and EM read `historic`; ST and MO read `modern`. NF and IS read `modern`, with 25% of names drawn from other cultures' `modern` lists, each culture weighted 3 if it is in the same lens family (British, Germanic) and 1 if not. Only cultures that have a `modern` table take part, so Anglo-Saxon never does.
- **General** reads Norman & British. **Norse** reads Scandinavian.
- **Anglo-Saxon** has only a `historic` table; in the steam age and later it reads Norman & British `modern`.
- **Celtic** with no region draws evenly from Welsh, Scottish, Irish and Cornish. Region WAL gives Welsh; COR gives Cornish; SBL, SLO, SHH and NSI give Scottish. Any other region leaves Celtic drawing evenly. The Welsh, Scottish and Cornish views therefore have tables of their own, so they now differ in every module that fills person slots.
- **Second tier:** each of the 22 lens cultures reads its own table. Roman and Egyptian have only a `historic` table (ancient peoples with no modern naming of their own): in the steam age and later Roman reads Italian and Egyptian reads Arabic and Persian. Aztec, Maya and Andean have a `modern` table of their own (shared tables brief §13.4): Spanish given names tagged by era, and the surnames each people carries now.
- **Local cultures** read the nearest second-tier culture: Maori, Hawaiian and Western Polynesian read Polynesian; Ottoman & Barbary reads Arabic and Persian plus the Turkish extra; Swahili & Omani and Phoenician read Arabic and Persian; Malay & Indonesian and Maritime South-East Asian read Indian historically and Arabic and Persian from the steam age; Hellenistic reads Greek and Byzantine; Belgian reads Dutch.
- **No nearest culture:** Pacific Northwest Coast, Arctic, North American Woodlands & Rivers, North American Woodlands & Plains, Pacific Northwest & Arctic, and Australia & New Guinea have no second-tier people table. They resolve to nothing, so their person brackets stay as they are, as native peoples' names do (brief §1.1).

## Notes for review

- **Patronymics.** Anglo-Saxon, Scandinavian (historic) and Welsh (historic) have no surname list: those cultures named people by patronymic, and the slot forms fall back to the given name (agreed in the review: given name only, no "son of" forms).
- **Weights.** Weights follow each list's order, not counts from records: the first five names weigh 10, the next seven 7, then 5, 3 and 2. The regnal and holy lists use 6, 4, 3 and 2. Names added at the review weigh 2 or 3. Cornish `historic` is flat: 5 for Kenny's Old Cornish names, 3 for the later set.
- **Royal and saintly names.** Given-name and surname lists mark royal and saintly bearers with `r` and `s`, at most a fifth of a list's names and, as the review added, at most a fifth of its weight (a test checks both). Cornish `historic` is exempt: the early Cornish record is largely saints, and the saints' names were personal names. Regnal and holy lists are royal or saintly by nature and are exempt.
- **Spelling.** Old English names are in modern English forms (Ethelfleda, Elgiva, Edburga, Aldgyth), with no `Ae-` or `Ea-` spellings. Welsh names keep Welsh spelling where that is the usual English form (Llywelyn, Owain, Rhys) and are anglicised elsewhere (Griffith, Madoc, Meredith). Irish names are anglicised (Conor, Dermot, Teigue, Rory) except where the Irish spelling is itself usual in English (Aoife, Niamh, Siobhan). German umlauts are `ae`, `oe`, `ue` and `ß` is `ss` (Mueller, Koenig, Juergen); Scandinavian names drop the marks (Bjorn, Soren, Sjoberg).
- **Swedish and Danish or Norwegian surnames.** Andersson and Andersen, Larsson and Larsen and the like are kept as the separate national spellings of one name. That is a judgement call against rule 1: one spelling per name would halve the surname list. Say if you want one spelling.
- **Legendary names kept from the templates.** Kenny's Anglo-Saxon template holds Hengest, Horsa, Wealhtheow and Beadohild, known from legend as much as record; they stay because every template name goes in.
- **Celtic overlaps.** Welsh, Scottish and Irish `modern` lists share some common British names (John, William, Mary); the differences are in the Gaelic and Welsh names and the surnames.
- **Cornish saints' names and the blocked list.** Cornish modern surnames keep Trevithick; the M6 `blocked` list must catch "Richard Trevithick".
- **No famous full names.** Given names and surnames are drawn separately. The `blocked` list of famous real full names arrives at M6.

## Second tier: decisions and changes from the rules (M3)

The second-tier tables were written against the rules of brief §12, so there is no earlier version to diff. What the rules shaped, per culture:

- **French:** one spelling per name (Etienne, Guillaume, Jacques); no living royals; the saints Denis and Martin flagged `s`, the kings Louis and Charles `r`.
- **Dutch:** surnames with their particles as one surname ("Van der Meer"); Beatrix left out of the regnal list as a name chiefly known from a living royal.
- **Iberian:** Spanish and Portuguese in one list in the usual English form (Alfonso, not Afonso; Felipe); Letizia and other living consorts left out.
- **Italian:** Romance surnames in the Italian form; Doge as a ruler form (an English word).
- **Roman:** historic only; `AN` names and a late-Roman `MD` group (Boethius, Cassiodorus, the Church Fathers, flagged `s`); surnames are the gentilicia; no gods.
- **Greek and Byzantine:** `AN` and `MD` tags as the brief says; modern Greek names in the usual English transliteration; "Saint", "Elder" and "Patriarch" for holy forms.
- **Slavic:** one pan-Slavic list across Russian, Polish, Czech and South Slavic names, with the Russian transliteration for East Slavic.
- **Turkic and Mongol steppe:** historic names are the Mongol and Turkic ones; modern names are Turkish (and Anatolian), the largest living Turkic population. Historic surnames none; Turkish modern surnames date from 1934.
- **Arabic and Persian:** historic has no surnames (nasab); the modern lists are Arabic and Persian names in one list. Rabia, Hallaj, Rumi and the like in the holy list; "Sheikh", "Imam" and "Sufi" as forms. Living royals (Rania, Farah) left out.
- **Egyptian:** historic only, `AN` for the pharaonic names and `MD` for Coptic ones; Coptic saints' names are given names, so not flagged. No gods.
- **Ethiopian:** no surnames in either period (patronymic naming).
- **Bantu:** southern and eastern African names together; no surnames historic. Historic women's names are thin in the record: Nandi, Mnkabayi, Manthatisi, Nzinga and Nehanda are attested, and the rest are Zulu and Xhosa given names of the early record.
- **West African:** Akan day names, Mande, Yoruba, Igbo, Hausa and Wolof; no surnames historic.
- **Indian:** historic has no surnames; modern surnames are the common regional ones. Gods' names (Lakshmi, Parvati, Radha) stay as given names borne by real women.
- **Chinese, Japanese, Korean:** surnames in both periods; the regnal lists use the dynastic and imperial names rulers took; living imperial figures left out (Michiko, Masako).
- **Aztec, Maya, Andean:** a `historic` and a `modern` table each (corrected on 15 November 2026, see "Changes after the fixes review"). The historic women's names are still thin in the record: please check these three first.
- **Polynesian:** Hawaiian, Maori, Samoan and Tongan together; no surname list (modern surnames are mostly European or very varied).
- **American:** colonial historic names; presidents' first names as regnal names (the monarch slot reads "President", "Governor" or "King"); modern surnames the common US ones.


## Changes after the fixes review (15 November 2026)

Shared tables brief §13.4. The §12 rules (one spelling per name, right sex, people only, rulers' and saints' share by weight, era tags) now hold for the Aztec, Maya and Andean tables.

### Aztec

- **historic, male given names**: 30 → 36; removed 16 (Tizoc, Cuitlahuac, Chimalpopoca, Acamapichtli, Huitzilihuitl, Nezahualpilli, Maxtla, Cacama, Ixtlilxochitl, Tezozomoc, Totoquihuatzin, Tlaltecatzin, Coanacoch, Tenoch, Tlacahuepan, Cuauhtlatoa); added 22 (Yaotl, Tochtli, Cuauhtli, Ocelotl, Ce Acatl, Ome Tochtli, Tlahuicole, Quiahuitl, Itzcuin, Coatl, Ehecatl, Chimalli, Tecpatl, Chicome Xochitl, Yei Calli, Ome Acatl, Chicuei Cuauhtli, Cuetzpalin, Macuil Ocelotl, Nahui Ollin, Matlactli Atl, Xipil).

- **Anglo-Saxon:** Hengest, Horsa, Wealhtheow and Beadohild (Kenny's template names) are at the lowest weight, 1.
- **Roman and Egyptian:** no modern period; the later eras read Italian and Arabic and Persian respectively.
- **Holy forms:** the Maya take "Ah Kin {n}" beside Priest, Seer and Sage; the Andean colonial saints only ever take "Saint {n}", and "Inca {m}" is never put on a name that holds Inca.

## Second-tier splits (brief 6a §3)

Spanish and Portuguese are cut from the Iberian tables (which stay, as the mix); Greek and Byzantine from the Greek-and-Byzantine tables (which stay, as the mix). Written by `scripts/make-tier6a-people.mjs` from `scripts/tier6a/people-data.mjs`; same rules as the shared tables brief §3 and its review amendments (one spelling per name, right sex, people only, rulers' and saints' share by weight, era tags, no living royals).

- **Spanish and Portuguese:** each has historic and modern tables; the modern lists carry the era tags `ST`, `MO` or both and the thirteen names given most in the last thirty years. Portuguese surnames and given names (Joao, Duarte, Goncalo, Silva, Pereira) are Portuguese only; the Iberian table keeps both.
- **Greek:** historic only (the ancient Greek names, tagged `AN`); the modern period reads Byzantine's modern table, which is the modern Greek one. No gods; seers and priestesses in the holy list.
- **Byzantine:** historic (tagged `MD`; the Byzantine families as surnames) and modern (the modern Greek names, as the Greek-and-Byzantine table).

## Second-tier splits (brief 6b §B4)

Turkic and Mongol are written for each culture (the steppe table stays, as the mix); Arabic and Persian likewise (arabic-persian stays, as the mix). Written by `scripts/make-tier6b-people.mjs` from `scripts/tier6b/people-data.mjs`. Famous figures (rulers, conquerors, poets, philosophers, scholars) are flagged ʳ, listed last and held to a fifth of a list's weight; ordinary attested names lead. The famous figures of Egyptian, Ethiopian, Bantu and West African are flagged the same way.

### Mappings

`ottoman` reads Turkic; `swahili-omani` and `phoenician` read Arabic; `malay` and `maritimeSEA` read Indian historic and Arabic modern; `egyptian` reads Egyptian historic and Arabic modern.

## Second-tier people tables, batch 6c (brief 6c §B1)

Indian, Chinese, Japanese and Korean, rewritten: ordinary attested names lead each list; rulers, generals, sages, poets and founders are flagged (ʳ) and held last, at a fifth or less; no god's name. Chinese, Japanese and Korean full names are family name first. Romanisation: pinyin; Hepburn without macrons; Revised Romanisation. Written by `scripts/make-tier6c-people.mjs` from `scripts/tier6c/people-data.mjs`.

## Second-tier people tables, batch 6d (brief 6d §B1)

Polynesian, Aztec, Maya, Andean and American, rewritten: ordinary attested names lead each list; rulers, generals and founders are flagged (ʳ) and held last, at a fifth or less; no god's name. Polynesian names keep each language's standard marks (Māori macrons, Hawaiian kahakō and the ʻokina), so the marked form is the one spelling. Spanish given names are written as the existing Spanish tables write them, without accents. The Aztec, Maya and Andean historic lists hold the native names with Spanish baptismal names tagged to the colonial eras (EM, ST). The American table covers the United States and Canada together (French-Canadian and other common Canadian given names and surnames added). **Needs a specialist's review:** the Polynesian lists (five languages), and the Aztec, Maya and Andean historic lists, especially the Nahuatl and Yucatec numeral-and-day-sign forms (the 260-day count, built from attested parts) and the Yucatec and Quechua given names; written from names I could stand behind, with no name invented to fill a gap. Written by `scripts/make-tier6d-people.mjs` from `scripts/tier6d/people-data.mjs`.
